This shows that even one province may be broken into two if necessity arises, and that Congress has now accepted this principle.

If Congress considers it necessary to divide Punjab for convenience, consciousness of rights, or protection, does it not follow that a plan to create divisions in India so that national states may arise for similar or other reasons is eminently appropriate?

There was a time when Congress spoke of India with intense devotion. When the British devised a plan to divide Bengal, Congress fought against the partition. Gone are the days when it advised, "Let Janab Jinnah rule the whole country if need be, but do not partition it." Those who opposed partition now demand that a province be split into two. What a marvellous transformation of mind!

It is said that Hindu and Sikh people can live safely only if Punjab is split into one region where Hindus and Sikhs are the majority and another where Muslims are the majority. If Punjab remains one province, majority rule will produce a Muslim government. Sikhs and Hindus cannot live under it, they say; hardship will result. The Working Committee therefore demands that the region in which Sikhs and Hindus form the majority be separated. It cites the communal riots in Punjab as the reason.

Janab Jinnah must be smiling to himself,

### Image 3

seeing those who opposed Pakistan advance so far as to demand Thundustans of their own. In some Punjab districts Hindus and Sikhs form the majority. On the two banks of the Sutlej, Hindus and Sikhs are the majority on one side and Muslims on the other.

The following Punjab districts are listed as having Hindu majorities:

| District | District |
|---|---|
| Gurgaon | Gurdaspur |
| Hisar | Hoshiarpur |
| Firozpur | Kangra |
| Ludhiana | Ambala |
| Jullundur | Karnal |
| Amritsar | Rohtak |

Congress proposes to separate these districts from Punjab and join them to the United Provinces. It says that the Hindus in these districts cannot be left under the League ministry that would be formed in Punjab.

Congress has therefore accepted that Punjab will have a League ministry. Earlier it said something different: "The League has no influence in Punjab; only a non-League ministry can be formed there." It is now clear that Congress itself has recognized that government in Punjab will fall to the League. It consequently declares that regions with large Hindu and Sikh populations cannot be left under League rule.

But Muslims will remain in several provinces governed by Hindus. Congress refuses to consider what answer should be given if they too demand separate regions within their provinces. Nor is that all. Although non-Muslims form the majority in the territory Congress wishes to detach from Punjab, Muslims are also present there in significant numbers. As though it were not enough that Muslims in Hindu-majority provinces must live under Hindu rule,

### Image 4

Congress now devises a plan by which Muslims in the part separated from Punjab must also submit to Hindu rule. Here are the figures:

| District | Muslim population |
|---|---:|
| Gurgaon | 33% |
| Hisar | 28% |
| Firozpur | 45% |
| Ludhiana | 36% |
| Jullundur | 45% |
| Amritsar | 46% |
| Gurdaspur | 51% |
| Hoshiarpur | 32% |
| Kangra | 4% |
| Ambala | 31% |
| Karnal | 30% |
| Rohtak | 17% |

These are the numbers of Muslims present, yet they are expected to live under Hindu rule. Why? Who can give a reason?

The Congress Working Committee believes that this much territory and this many non-Muslims must not remain under Muslim rule. Yet it appears never to consider that many crores of Muslims in the provinces of Bombay, the Central Provinces, the United Provinces, Madras, and Bihar must live under non-Muslim rule.

Muslims in such great numbers are expected to submit to Hindu rule, and are said to consent to it. Under the present arrangement, Janab Jinnah himself must live not in Pakistan but in Bombay. Even so, Congress advances to the point of breaking every Muslim province into pieces on the ground that non-Muslims must not live under Muslim rule.

### Image 5

This new plan has raised its head not only in Punjab, but everywhere.

Jairamdas Daulatram, a prominent Congressman, is said to have drawn up and circulated among the leaders a plan concerning Sindh. Because Hindus form the majority in Karachi and several neighbouring districts, he argues, they must not be handed over to the Muslim government that may arise in Sindh. Those districts should be separated from Sindh and made into an independent unit.

In Bengal, the president of the provincial Hindu Mahasabha has published a plan to detach Lower Bengal from Bengal and turn it into Hindu territory. The following Bengal districts are proposed for separation:

| District | District |
|---|---|
| Birbhum | Midnapore |
| Bankura | 24 Parganas |
| Burdwan | Khulna |
| Hooghly | |

#### Why Should We Be Drawn into the North's Troubles?

Thus plans are now being prepared to break the provinces into pieces in Punjab, Sindh, and Bengal. The new plan of the very men who once asked, in voices mingling fury and lament, "Partition India?" no longer merely rejects Pakistan; it seeks to mutilate and diminish it.

Whether this will lead to the conclusion that the rule of one people is utterly intolerable to another can be considered later. The lesson already obtained is that there is no longer any place for the fantasy of one government. And is that all? The northern situation is being drawn into this poisonous vortex of partition within partition, and miniature provinces within provinces.

### Image 6

The South, which has no need whatever to be caught in this vortex, is forced into it merely because it has been placed in Section A. Is this good? Is it necessary for us? Must we become embroiled in northern quarrels and lose our prosperity?

Why should we not say: "Resolve your territorial disputes by whatever method you choose. Dravidam is naturally separate, capable of living independently, and need not become a breeding ground for disputes in which lives are at stake. Do not place it in Section A and entangle us in your quarrels. We wish to live separately and shall constitute ourselves as an independent state"?

Why should our Congress comrades not prevent the South from being caught and ruined in the dreadful vortex arising in the North? This is no mere question and no rebuttal from an opposition party. It is the appeal addressed to Dravidian comrades in Congress by every Dravidian youth who has lost sleep in anxiety over the future.

Friends! Resolve today that, for the country to recover and for us to live, Dravidam must become an independent state. We want no bond with Section A.

The condition, history, and mentality of the northern people have become such that quarrels and coercion flourish. The League began by saying that the country must not be ruled by one people and that it required a state of its own. Today, in the North, Congress presses a plan to divide province after province.

The League does not participate in the Constituent Assembly. Britain says the Assembly's decision cannot be implemented without the League. Assam refuses to participate in the meetings of Sections B and C. Congress leaders in the Frontier and the Sikhs of Punjab also refuse. The sectional meetings therefore do not convene; and even if they meet and devise plans, Britain says those plans cannot be imposed on regions that did not participate.

This difficulty grows and matures,

### Image 7

until the leaders themselves are bewildered and see no path forward. Choosing precisely that moment, the British say: "We shall remain only sixteen more months and then leave. Reach some decision before then; otherwise we shall establish whatever arrangement seems expedient to us and depart."

This may be called intimidation, or even mockery. When every proposed arrangement has already intensified mutual bitterness and hostility, it can be maintained only through force. There is no other way. And the brief but terrifying name for that is civil war. Is the country's present degradation not enough? What does civil war mean? Is it not the condition of China, now being ruined?

Is this what all that "sacrifice" is to be used for? If such a condition arises, what can happen except that the country once again becomes the hunting ground of great powers? Instead of withdrawing from this trouble, why entangle Dravidam, which possesses every facility, right, and necessity for independence? Our already diminished land will lose all form and disintegrate if we are forcibly bound to the North. Why should our Congress friends not recognize this in advance?

#### Krishna-Karim Is Not Enough!

We have shown the condition toward which they are dragging the North, already turned into a land of disorder. Walking through the fields and narrow paths of Noakhali, Gandhi added two new lines to the familiar hymn "Raghupati Raghava Raja Ram, Patita Pavana Sita Ram":

"Ishwar Allah Tero Nam,
Sabko Sanmati De Bhagavan."

The meaning is: "O God! Ishwar and Allah are both Your names. Grant all of us sound understanding and guide us along the right path." There is also another song:

### Image 8

"Bhaj man pyare, Ram-Rahim;
Bhaj man pyare, Krishna-Karim."

Ram-Rahim! Krishna-Karim!

Gandhi thus presents them joined together. Is this a new method? No. Teachers of reconciliation have emphasized the same point for a very long time. No one has denied that although people worship under many different names, God is one. Nor has anyone failed to draw attention to it in times of crisis.

But such teachings of reconciliation have never, in any age, dispelled the suspicions arising between two peoples in the political, economic, and social spheres. The dispute did not begin over whether the name is Rama or Rahim. Are not the real questions Congress or League, imperial rule or two governments? The adhesive spell "Krishna-Karim" is not enough to resolve them.

Gandhi gave courage to Hindus, showed a path toward Hindu-Muslim unity, and asked Hindus not to leave Bengal. Now he has gone to Bihar to console Muslims and ask them not to leave Bihar, while advising Bihar's Hindus to behave in a manner that gives Muslims courage and confidence. Punjab too offers Gandhi work.

Would it be responsible and loving to keep assigning him forever the task of applying medicine to wounds? Why does such a condition continue to grow there? The northern environment is a major cause. We ask you to consider calmly why our land should be joined to that region and ruined. An explanation and a path will appear only when thought is allowed to move beyond party bonds.

We make an appeal to our Dravidian comrades in Congress. Friends! You said: "India is one nation. Its rule must therefore be constituted as one government. It is wrong to speak of North and South within it. We require all-India politics, and so we must remain in the All-India Congress."

### Image 9

Your argument may have been valid until yesterday. What is the position today? Many parties, including Congress, have abandoned the principle of one government. If the League's plan succeeds, the Congress system of government will apply only to the regions excluding the Frontier, Punjab, Sindh, Bengal, and Assam.

Even under the new Congress plan intended to weaken the League's proposal, the Frontier and Assam will stand separately, one part of Punjab separately, one part of Sindh separately, and one part of Bengal separately. Can this caricature be called "All India"? Is this Bharatavarsha?

Whichever of these two plans succeeds, the land that results will not be the ideal territory you envisioned when joining Congress. It will not extend from the Himalayas in the north to Kanyakumari in the south. The boundary of that ideal land has changed.

"Upon the banks of the Indus,
With a maiden of the noble Chera land,
Singing melodies in beautiful Telugu,
We shall launch our boats and play."

There is no path by which this poet's dream can become reality, whether events follow the League plan or the Congress plan, because there is no longer an all-India political scheme. Congress has agreed that the country may be divided into Sections A, B, and C and governments formed accordingly.

All of Congress's present efforts are not directed toward abolishing Sections A, B, and C, but toward determining how much the two Muslim sections, B and C, can be reduced. Whether Congress will reduce them, or remain obstinate and then yield at the last moment as it has on many other questions, we do not know. Whatever happens, it is now established that there is no whole India, no single India.

Once this is clear, Congress friend, why should we still retain a connection with the All-India Congress? It is a somewhat uncomfortable question,

### Image 10

but as the days pass it will inevitably stir thought.

Once it has become clear that an all-India political scheme cannot be created, what reason can comrades in the South give for claiming that association with the All-India Congress remains necessary? Think about it without anger.

Do not ask, "Are you playing Punnagavarali and calling upon everyone to join the Dravidar Kazhagam immediately?" Friends, remain a separate party if you wish; we find no fault in that. We ask only this: after the all-India political scheme has collapsed, why do you remain connected with the All-India Congress? What benefit does it bring?

Perhaps it brings no benefit. You might console yourselves by saying that affection has not released its hold. But the connection not only lacks benefit; it causes harm. Why retain such a relationship? Is there any reason? There may have been one before, friend. But today, after all-India politics has ceased to exist and after the country has become A, B, and C? Consider it.

Do not imagine that a net is being cast. I merely draw your attention to the subtle point stated by Periyar, the leader of the Dravidar Kazhagam, on many platforms: "If the connection with the North is severed, my comrades and I are prepared to join the Congress of the South."

Do not therefore regard this as an attempt to recruit people into a party. I say it because the unnecessary and profitless party division among us must end.

What is now taking place in the North is the Congress working to reduce the Muslim Sections B and C, and the League working to preserve B and C according to its plan. Neither side is working to prevent the country from being divided into the three Sections A, B, and C. That effort has already been attempted and abandoned. What is now taking place there...

## Part 5

### Image 1

is merely the addition of a few more districts to Hindustan by reducing the number of districts within Pakistan. That is the work this country is pursuing. It resembles broadening a ridge in one's own field until it advances into the neighbouring field. What work have we in this?

Once matters have reached this stage, why should comrades in the South think of retaining a connection with an all-India party? Why should they desire it? What meaning, benefit, or necessity does it possess? I ask you to think and reach a sound conclusion.

Every Congress friend accepts that Congress's work is nearly complete. Congress existed to defeat imperialism, did it not? Very well. Once imperialism, deprived of its fundamental strength, says that it is packing its bags, has not Congress's basic task succeeded? Did you not join Congress to abolish imperialism? Imperialism itself is to disappear in 1948. What service remains to be performed from within Congress?

You will say: "Is obtaining self-rule enough? Must it not be put in order? Must the country not be reformed? Must we not prevent reactionaries from entering into power? Have we no work until the weakened country regains its splendour? Congress still has work: it must enrich the country it has recovered."

That is a reasonable argument, a necessary statement, and an undeniable reason. I do not reject it. These things must indeed be done. Only then will we obtain good government and the good life produced by it. I reject none of that argument. But I ask one question, friend.

Why is an all-India party required to reform the country, create prosperity, avoid entanglement with troublemakers, and perform all these other good works? Is it not proper to have a party for the land in which you live, with a programme suited to it? To perform the necessary tasks you rightly desire,

### Image 2

in what way is association with the All-India Congress essential? Think and answer. Do not answer for my sake, nor hesitate to speak the truth merely because I ask. For the sake of your own country, say what your heart tells you. Why remain bound to an all-India party that has abandoned the all-India ideal, when your purpose is to perform the good work of reforming your own land? What necessity, benefit, or meaning does it possess?

#### Why the Ganges When We Have the Kaveri?

What possible connection can exist between the tasks that must now be performed here and the North? Although our own people were born in this land and possess the right to live here, our failure to provide opportunities has left many lakhs of them in the degrading condition of labouring until their bones melt in distant and unseen lands such as Malaya, Ceylon, Zanzibar, and Mauritius. We must bring them back and give them a life.

To do this, our country must be enriched. Are we to enrich it with water from the Ganges? We have the Kaveri, Vaigai, and South Pennar. Beautiful fields lie on both banks. We have forests and the useful wealth within them. What do we lack, and for what resource must we yearn and look toward the North? Why should the work to be done on the Kaveri's banks require a connection with the banks of the Ganges?

Do not be angry, friend; think. A thousand afflictions exist here. Why do we need a northern connection or association with an all-India party to resolve them? If ours were a land without resources, means, capacity for thought, ability to act, or an ancient history, it would be proper to seek assistance and connections. But ours is not in that degraded condition.

Everything is here, including people capable of doing anything. Two thousand years ago this country produced literature of a kind no other people had discovered. In ancient times it established commerce with the Yavanas and connections with China. What can we say we lack today that would require a connection with the North?

### Image 3

The League of Nations was once established to preserve peace in the world, and many countries joined it. Mussolini and Hitler arose and shattered the League. War broke out. Today not only is the war gone; the ineffectual League is gone too.

The All-India Congress existed to bring all India under one umbrella. Today Congress itself has realized that an all-India government is impossible and has altered its practice accordingly. Under these circumstances, why should the South retain an all-India connection?

Good work must be done to reform the country; only then will the benefit of self-rule become visible. But no northern connection is required to perform that work. The work we must do must be suited to our country's condition, culture, needs, and facilities. An all-India party is unnecessary for it. Why, then, retain one?

Do not be displeased that I ask questions enough to bewilder you, friend. You may feel moved to ask: "If merely recognizing this would lead them to dissolve Congress, why have they not done so? Do you think they understand nothing?" That too is a reasonable question.

The North still requires Congress. It has a need and work for it. Congress is required to perform two important tasks, though they embrace each other so closely that they can scarcely be separated. One is to prevent Muslim domination. The other is to protect the flourishing capitalist order from collapse. They have planned to use Congress in the North for both purposes.

That is why members of the Congress Socialist Party such as Jayaprakash, Aruna Asaf Ali, and Achyut Patwardhan openly say, "Within Congress..."

### Image 4

"the Dalmias and Bajajs exercise domination." Northern capitalists have already seized the propaganda machinery so that North Indian Congress may continue to serve this purpose.

Under these conditions, why should we remain connected to North Indian Congress?

Why should we purchase trouble at a price?

Even a hand that held honey must be washed after the honey has been eaten; otherwise tiny ants will bite it. Dalmia, Bajaj, and the others are no tiny ants. If capitalism stings, our already weakened country cannot endure it.

That is why I say that Dravidians in Congress can no longer claim that a northern connection and an all-India party remain necessary. They have no meaning, necessity, or benefit.

#### A New Problem Requires a New Method

"Does that mean no party is needed at all?" you ask. A party is needed. From today onward, the country has an abundance of good work to perform. Thousands upon thousands of social and economic problems require resolution.

We must remove the cruelty of caste running rampant among Tamils who once knew no caste; stop poverty from dancing its savage dance among Tamils who once knew no poverty; awaken sleeping nature; set idle wealth into motion; enable the languishing worker to live; and bring home our kinsmen who suffer in many directions.

Friend, as one reflects, countless tasks appear before the mind's eye, each quarrelling, "I come first! No, I do!" We must welcome science. We must create a path to life for comrades who, because life on earth has been wounded, stand raising their hands toward heaven. Society must be melted down and cast anew. A party is needed for this work.

### Image 5

As these tasks are undertaken, parties will form along reactionary, radical, and moderate lines. Dravidian comrades in Congress, come forward to organize a party on that basis. When you do so, first examine whether the Dravidar Kazhagam possesses a policy for such work. If it does, join it. Why merely join, friends? Take possession of it! If you believe it does not, form another party. I find no fault with that.

But remove the unwanted, unprofitable, unnecessary, and trouble-producing connection with the All-India Congress, and with it the northern connection obtained through that Congress.

British imperialism will leave in 1948. Bring about a condition in which, on that same auspicious day, the South too resolves to shine in its separate sovereignty, devises a plan for it, and comes forward to perform the good work needed for the country's progress, severing the northern and all-India party connection because it recognizes that neither is required for this task.

"I am packing my bags! I shall leave in June 1948. Enough of this pointless trouble!"

White imperialism has announced this publicly, before all the world, in the Parliament that rules it. Prime Minister Attlee's declaration has announced that the imperialism which seized and tormented India for the past two centuries is ending.

Joy! Boundless joy! Tamil! Comrade!

The burdens loaded upon Tamils have bent their lives. Aryanism, mythology, caste, northern capitalism, and many others weigh upon them. Carrying all these burdens has left Tamil life bowed.

White imperialism is leaving India. Freedom is being obtained.

Will Aryanism release the Tamil? Will freedom be obtained?

### Image 6

Are there no heroes who will undertake the effort to obtain freedom?

It is said that when just the uppermost of the many loads carried by a camel is removed, the animal imagines its burden has been reduced, rises from where it lay, and begins to walk. Poor creature! It does not realize that many more loads remain on its back.

Tamil! They say that in 1948 one burden - white imperialism - will be cast down.

Is that enough? Unless the other burdens strangling our lives are also cast down, there is no life. Come forward to labour for life and freedom. Victory is ours; fear not!

Lord Mountbatten, arriving as the last Viceroy, is said to be bringing two plans. One sets out how power should be transferred if Congress and the League reach a settlement. The other sets out how the British should act if the two parties fail to come to terms until the very end.

An Invitation to That Lawrence!

Unless this second plan is rendered unnecessary, no path will open for the country's progress. The country's condition is deteriorating to the point where both parties consult omens about which side British strength will join. Would Britain act without calculating which side offers it profit?

Think now and it will become clear: if the measures taken in the days of the present Lawrences are examined closely, they prepare the way for the old policy of Stringer Lawrence, who entered a civil conflict and acquired the right to rule the country.

This must be prevented, and it can be prevented. But under the present northern conditions, there is no way for clarity to arise.

### Image 7

The League may believe that Britain must support it in view of its connections with Muslim countries across the world. Congress may likewise think Britain will support it in view of its growing commercial and economic strength and alliances. Whichever party's plan Britain supports, what will be the result? The return of the British grip.

What if Britain supports neither party and says, "Resolve your domestic quarrels yourselves"? The condition of China: disintegration.

#### Why Should Dravidam Be Worn Away?

Why should Dravidam be worn away through a connection that has no relevance or necessity for it and can only bring harm? Dravidian comrades in Congress must examine this with the greatest seriousness. If the country's future is to be prosperous, we must clarify our course now.

* * *

#### Curzon - Carson!

"Down with Curzon! Down with Carson!

"Down with the demons of partition!

"We will not let Mother Bengal be cut apart! We will not let the foreigner's plan succeed!

"We will not let Mother Ireland be cut apart! We will not let the unjust Carson triumph!"

Thus cried those driven by nationalism, once upon a time.

Curzon and Carson were both caught in the fiery vortex raised in nationalism's name.

### Image 8

Curzon was Viceroy of the Indian subcontinent. For administrative convenience, he devised a plan to turn Bengal into two provinces. Angered by it, the Bengali movement, the Vande Mataram movement, the Swadeshi movement, and the Congress movement successively acquired the power of popular resistance.

Carson devised a plan to establish one portion of Ireland, the region called Ulster, as a separate state.

In Ireland, the "patriots" condemned Carson. In India, patriots condemned Curzon.

Curzon's plan did not succeed then. Carson's plan - the partition of Ireland and the creation of a separate Ulster government - succeeded at that very time.

Curzon has now taken his revenge. Congressmen today seek to implement the same plan to partition Bengal that they once opposed and defeated.

Just as Carson demanded and obtained Ulster, Pakistan is demanded here. It has been refused, yet continues to take shape.

Both Curzon and Carson have gained influence here today, many years after their deaths.

The plan spoken by the grandson into the ear of the Bengali Babu today is, "Divide Bengal in two." Even Curzon's stone image must laugh mockingly at this condition.

The English entered among a fragmented empire, declining powers, and princely states tangled in complications, and thereby acquired the opportunity to rule the country. Although military force won victories for them in many regions, statecraft was the principal reason they remained here.

The British were not somehow the world's foremost invincible heroes, nor were the peoples living across the Indian subcontinent cowards. The sidelong glance of a maiden...

### Image 9

may have felled men in the flavour of epic poetry, but many battlefields here have also marvelled at the sweep of the sword in the hands of our own people. The English did not subdue the powerless. We did not lose our land to warriors; we lost it to tacticians who turned our futile quarrels to their advantage.

At that time the Europeans did not possess the modern instruments of war they have today. They carried rifles like those now used to shoot cranes. Men boasting descent from the Ikshvaku line, the Solar dynasty, Ranabhadrakali, and Viravaku; the Palas, Senas, Kadambas, Rashtrakutas, Rajputs, Marathas, Mughals, and Sikhs - bearers of so many titles - proved unable to withstand men carrying bird-shooting rifles.

What could be done? Gunpowder yields nothing when water has been poured over it. Courage had been dimmed through association with the worthless. That is why the English, who first pitched tents, later attained the position of sitting enthroned.

They had to reorganize the many scattered governments: join them, cut them apart, melt them down, and cast them anew. At first they remained in the port regions of Madras, Bombay, and Calcutta; later they established governments in all those places.

When forming provinces, they paid no attention to ethnicity, language, or culture. Why would they possess such concern? Their sole object was an orderly administration suited to governance, and especially to military operations. They converted many regions into "administrative units," changing those arrangements from time to time according to emerging difficulties and circumstances. That work is still unfinished.

We have witnessed many changes within our own country, matters worth recalling.

Older Congressmen will remember the occasion when, viewing India as one nation,

### Image 10

they personified the country as a goddess and published her image. An old copy may still be found somewhere. In it, Burma appears as the loose end of the goddess's sari. What of today?

The loose end is gone. Burma was separated and made an independent state. The cry "Burma for the Burmese" was not heard for even ten full years before the Burmese obtained their separate sovereignty.

Even those who had drawn Burma as the end of the goddess's sari and sung the praises of that image saw it become an independent state and finally said, "Very well, it is their wish." At first they angrily asked, "Can Burma be separated?" Then they declared indignantly, "We shall not permit Burma to be separated." Later they asked sorrowfully, "Must Burma really separate?" Still later they stood in tears and said, "Burma has separated after all." Thus the pitch of their voice changed step by step.

As soon as what had happened became an accomplished fact, the pitch began changing again: "Though separated, our desire is that Burma should prosper"; "What if it has separated? We shall not forget it"; "Separation has not destroyed our affection"; "Burma is our companion, friend, and relative"; "Who else can possess the concern we have for Burma's joys and sorrows?"

Today the situation has changed so far, and friendship become so firmly established, that newspapers write with delight of the Aung San-Nehru embrace.

Burma was once treated simply as a part of India. There was a time when even imagining that Burma might become a separate country was denounced as the work of a traitor.

Separated Burma can teach us a lesson if we study its history thoughtfully.

Burma, which remained joined to India until 1937, became an independent country. No disaster appears to have occurred. Merchant princes may have lost part of their hunting ground, but the people suffered no loss. Nor did separation prevent friendship and relationship from arising.

## Part 6

### Image 1

To think, "We must never separate; if we separate, we must become enemies," is neither wisdom nor a practical truth of politics. Once the desire to separate has arisen, compelling people to remain united is the greater danger. It is a path that cultivates hatred, a vessel of poison that such a country, through our obstinacy, leaves behind for later generations.

Burma's total population is 17 million. Burma, with fewer than two crore people, is an independent country. Since when? Since 1937.

Burma was one of the territories the English joined and cut apart after entering the Indian landmass. British power gradually grew in Burma. In 1826 Burma was joined to India and brought under the Delhi government. For 110 years, from 1826 to 1937, Burma was ruled by the "Government of India." No one here considered this unnecessary or unjust. People rejoiced that Burma too was part of Bharatavarsha.

Even that is not surprising. No one even in Burma thought, "We have lost our separate government and been joined to India." In 1937 Burma separated. Today they have become Burmese once again. To use the old picture, the loose end of the goddess's sari is gone; Aung San possesses that piece of cloth. Pandit Nehru is not angered by the sight. The two are excellent friends.

Just as Burma was a province of the Indian government, it also held a place in the All-India Congress. Just as every province now has a Congress committee, Burma had a Provincial Congress Committee. Congress members in other regions greeted leaders arriving from Burma with the same enthusiastic joy with which they greet the Frontier Gandhi today. Buddhist monks in Burma worked actively in the Congress movement,

### Image 2

went to prison, and became prominent leaders known as Burmese patriots. Senior Congressmen will remember. Such was the intimacy created in Congress's name.

The Burma Congress and its parent body, the All-India Congress, agitated on the position that Burma must not separate, Bharatavarsha must not be partitioned, the proposal was a British conspiracy, and they must not fall victim to it. Later, step by step, Burma's union with India came to be described as a conspiracy that consumed Burma's wealth. The very people in Burma who had supported Congress began to say, "Burma belongs to the Burmese; this alone is true nationalism," and supported separation.

Burma separated. The All-India Congress does not now demand that it be reunited with India. It possesses no such intention.

Even a survey of several provinces shows that this joining and cutting is neither new nor something that should make us tremble at the very mention of it. Under the new plan, are not the Frontier Province, Punjab, and Sindh together to form Section B? What does the Frontier Gandhi say? "What! Shall the Frontier be placed under Punjab's domination? What injustice! My brave Pathans will never desire it."

But do the Pathans truly desire what the Frontier Gandhi claims? There is no sign of it. The available evidence points in the opposite direction. Even nationalist newspapers report that groups of Pathans are "invading" Punjab. Why? To assist the Muslims there.

The Frontier Gandhi is alarmed at the prospect of Punjab and the Frontier being joined, and nationalist comrades here are alarmed with him. But consider a little history. Through the British method of "joining and cutting," Punjab and the Frontier remained one province until 1901. Curzon separated the Frontier into its own province for military convenience.

### Image 3

Nor is that all. History in many northern regions resembles railway carriages left separately on a line and coupled at different times to different engines. Whichever direction the attached engine travels, the carriages follow.

When Mahmud of Ghazni conquered northern India, he separated the conquered territory from India and ruled it from Ghazni, his capital. The territory then included what are now the Frontier and Punjab. Until then it had been joined to the Indian landmass; it was cut away and joined to the kingdom of Ghazni. That joining was later cut.

Muhammad of Ghor ruled the same territory first from Lahore and later from Delhi, rather than from Ghazni. At that time it was again joined to India.

Under British domination, the same region remained a single province joined to India from 1849 to 1901.

Until a few years ago Sindh was joined to Bombay. During that period several attempts were made to join Sindh with Punjab. The two regions are neighbours, and people of the same ethnicity formed, and still form, the majority in both. A river itself provides a natural connection between Punjab and Sindh.

Why should regions with such convenient links, including the Frontier and Punjab areas that remained one province for so long, be denied a place under Pakistan's flag?

The British later altered many of the unions they had created for administrative convenience and established new arrangements for that same reason. It was they who joined Burma to India.

### Image 4

That unnecessary act later brought danger, not to the British but to our own people. The Burmese knife struck not the white man, but the white man's Chettiars. In 1937 the British cut apart the same Burma they had once joined. Between those acts Burmese blood and Tamil blood were shed.

A general truth emerges from these varied events. The coercive method of forcing regions that cannot unite to remain together kindles not friendship but hatred. Good counsel cannot extinguish that hatred. When hatred matures, the animal sleeping within man emerges and man becomes enslaved to animal feeling. There in Noakhali, Bihar, Punjab, and the Frontier, the human animal spills fresh blood. The foreigner watches. What will he think?

If illness causes a body to swell, is that bodily growth? If a blood clot protrudes from the body, would only a fool say, "We have acquired a new lump of flesh that others do not possess; therefore it is a gain"? In the same way, the systems of one India, central government, and provinces constructed by imperialism for its own comfortable existence are neither a gain nor a source of strength for us.

Even if regions so ruined that they cannot unite are our ancestral property, we must let them go. We must protect and enrich what we possess: the place that remains united and gives confidence that unity can be maintained, thereby giving its people a life. We urgently require this political wisdom now.

The propaganda conducted until now both against and in support of partition can only have aroused hatred.

### Image 5

Platforms resounded with tales of each people's heroic prowess, the glory of crowned ancestors, victories over enemies, forts built, and flags flown. The ember became a fire. That is why ordinary people in the North must now suffer beyond endurance.

Had Pakistan been treated as Burma was - opposed to some extent and then accepted - today's disorder would not have occurred. A Jinnah-Gandhi friendship could have arisen like the Aung San-Nehru embrace. Even now, there is no need to abandon hope on the ground that the situation has been completely ruined.

Whether knowingly or unknowingly, amid all this turmoil the fundamental question is being resolved. The issue is no longer whether Pakistan should be granted. It has become a question of size: whether all Punjab and all Bengal should lie within Pakistan.

It is proposed that one part of Punjab must be cut away...

### Image 6

for the sake of the Sikhs. What proportion of India's total population do you think those Sikhs constitute? One in a hundred. A separate political right is deemed necessary for the welfare of this minority.

The differences between Muslim and Sikh need not even be as great as those between Hindu and Muslim. Neither Muslims nor Sikhs practise image worship. Neither possesses caste or clan divisions. History, however, records wars fought between them.

To protect the welfare and rights of Sikhs, who form one person in a hundred, it is proposed that the part of Punjab where they form the majority be separated and joined to a Hindu-inhabited region. If this appears necessary, what was wrong when Muslims, who form more than thirty per cent of the total population, asked that their own government should operate in the places where they are the majority? Why was partition opposed until hatred between the two peoples rose so high?

While coming forward with such concern to protect the rights of one person in a hundred, why must the more than four crore Dravidian people alone be joined to the North like orphaned children admitted to an institution? They governed themselves until the British rifle arrived; they differ in language and culture and possess ancient greatness and literary distinction.

Why should Dravidam be given in adoption to northern commerce? Our voice may lack strength, but does it lack justice? Is there no place here for fairness? On what ground do Dravidian comrades today believe they must remain in Congress and retain an all-India connection? Is Dravidam a directionless child with no one to seek it? Must it perform menial work behind whoever calls it? Dravidian comrades in Congress must consider these questions.

How easily Congress has declared that one part of Punjab must be separated. Why do Dravidian Congress comrades lack that courage?

Let us now examine the subtle purpose contained in the demand to divide Punjab.

Sikhs believe that division will enable them to avoid Muslim rule. Hindus believe that division will reduce the territory under Muslim rule while enlarging the Hindu region.

### Image 7

Another problem lies hidden within. It will emerge later: what relationship should exist between Hindus and Sikhs in the divided Punjab?

If Punjab remains one undivided province, Muslims will receive the first honour, but Sikhs will hold second place. In both numbers and historical importance, the Sikhs rank next to Muslims in Punjab.

If Punjab is divided and its detached part joined to the United Provinces or to Section A, Sikh influence will be smaller. Sikh leaders may take some time to understand this, but they will understand it.

It is said that Congress's demand to divide Punjab is intended to make the League grieve: "Has this fate not arisen because we demanded India's partition?" Congress supposedly hopes the League will then abandon its demand for Pakistan. League leaders who understand political strategy will instead use this situation to their own advantage.

Congressmen once cried in alarm, "The Leaguers demand that the country be cut apart!" and made the world feel disgust toward the League. Today that disgust is being removed, with Congress itself leading the work. It has entered upon this course without concern for the consequences.

Will the world now say that Muslims alone demand partition? They at least seek to divide a great subcontinent, though this was denounced as a great sin. Congressmen now cut apart even a single province.

Dividing the provinces as Congress demands is not impossible. Although the League would suffer some loss,

### Image 8

one cannot say that League leaders skilled in statecraft will reject it outright.

The question has passed beyond whether Pakistan will or will not exist. It has reached the stage of asking whether Pakistan will be this large or that large.

Even if its size is somewhat reduced, Leaguers will henceforth work with the conviction that their ideal of political rights has triumphed.

By losing one portion of Punjab, the League gains the opportunity to govern the greater part of Punjab exclusively, while also reducing its minority problem. In one respect, this is a gain.

To show how politically astute people have acted on such occasions, we offer a historical example.

The League's plan is that one part of India should become Pakistan. Congress's present plan is that one part of Pakistan should be detached and Pakistan's size reduced.

Similarly, a movement arose to turn one part of Ireland into a separate state called Ulster. Its leader was Sir Edward Carson.

Ireland itself is a small country. Should a region comprising roughly one quarter of it be separated and made an independent state? "No Ulster!" opponents declared against Carson's plan.

Opposition did not extinguish the partition movement.

They then stirred opposition within Ulster itself. Ulster comprised nine counties, three of which resisted inclusion in the Ulster polity.

This became a perplexing problem for Carson.

### Image 9

To obtain Ulster, he had to lose three counties. If he resolved that not one of the three could be lost, the establishment of an independent Ulster would be jeopardized. What was to be done?

Sir Edward Carson wavered between ideal and practicality. Then he reached a courageous decision: he consented to lose the three counties that refused to join Ulster. Ulster was obtained. Even today, it is the dream of Ulster that disturbs de Valera's sleep.

Janab Jinnah today faces a problem like the one that confronted Sir Carson. Even his opponents recognize Jinnah's political judgement. We can therefore understand, to some extent, what may occur.

Whether Pakistan is reduced in size or obtained in the full measure demanded, the idea of one India has disintegrated.

Under these circumstances, we ask: why should we, why should Dravidam, retain a connection with the North?

* * *

## Translator's Notes

This draft covers all ten scanned pages in Part 1. The final sentence intentionally continues into Part 2, as it does in the source. It is not yet a publishable bilingual document.

"World arbiters" renders உலகப் பஞ்சாயத்தார், a rhetorical expression invoking an international council or body of outside adjudicators.

"Statesmanship is their atom bomb" preserves the source's political metaphor for ராஜதந்திரம்.

The source deliberately shifts registers in the Sanatanist quotation, using colloquial, caste-marked speech. The English preserves its abusive tone without treating it as neutral narration.

This draft covers all ten scanned pages in Part 2. It begins and ends with sentences continued across the adjacent parts.

"Merchant princes" renders வியாபாரக் கோமான்கள். Parrys and Simpsons refer to commercial houses; Lawrence and Mayo invoke imperial administrators within Anna's contrast between old and new forms of control.

"A Peacock's Neck" preserves மயில் கழுத்து as the governing metaphor for the shifting and difficult-to-classify English character.

The isolated source reading “45” in Image 9 is retained literally because its intended wording remains uncertain. It should be checked again against the original print before publication.

"The Lawrence who hunted" is a conservative rendering of வேட்டுவிட்ட லாரன்சு; the printed phrase is rhetorically paired with உறவுகொண்டாடும் லாரன்சு, "the Lawrence who celebrates kinship."

This draft covers all ten scanned pages in Part 3. It begins and ends with sentences continued across adjacent parts.

“Commonwealth” renders சாம்ராஜ்யம் in the quoted proposal because the passage specifically concerns continued membership after formal independence; the surrounding narration retains “empire” where appropriate.

The damaged non-prose OCR fragments at the Image 4 page join are not translated. The surrounding printed sentence is continuous and clear.

துண்டுஸ்தான் is a satirical coinage built from துண்டு (fragment/piece) and the suffix in Hindustan/Pakistan. “Thundustan” is retained to preserve the wordplay; “The Fragmented-Land Plan” is its explanatory sense.

The source prints `T. A. Shone`; the name and the paired surnames “Cotton and Paton” are retained as printed from the verified scan.

This draft covers all ten scanned pages in Part 4. It begins and ends with sentences continued across adjacent parts.

Historical province and district names are rendered in period-appropriate English forms. The source's duplicated or variant geographic logic is preserved rather than reconciled to modern boundaries.

The source abbreviations in the province list are expanded conservatively as Central Provinces and United Provinces based on the verified scan and period usage.

The interfaith hymn lines are transliterated and then explained because their paired divine names are central to Anna's argument.

The four-line poem is by Subramania Bharati. The Tamil source remains verbatim in the final bilingual document; the English preserves its geographical and linguistic imagery.

புன்னாகவராளி is retained as Punnagavarali, the Carnatic raga invoked sarcastically as a plaintive recruiting call.

This draft covers all ten scanned pages in Part 5. It begins with a sentence continued from Part 4; Part 6 continues the Burma argument.

Yavana is retained in the historical sense used in Tamil literary discourse for western/Greek-Roman trading peoples.

The honey and ants analogy is translated literally because its escalation from tiny ants to industrial capitalists carries the satire.

The Curzon-Carson heading and slogans rely on the near rhyme of the two names. Their historical distinction is retained rather than regularized.

The source's “loose end of the goddess's sari” metaphor for Burma is preserved because Part 6 continues to develop it.

This draft covers all nine scanned pages in Part 6 and completes the work's English translation.

The phrase “the white man's Chettiars” preserves Anna's wording for Tamil commercial communities exposed to violence in Burma; it is not generalized or silently modernized.

The railway carriage metaphor retains the source's repeated “joining and cutting” vocabulary for changing political boundaries.

“First honour” renders முதல் தாம்பூலம், a ceremonial idiom for precedence; the following “second place” preserves Anna's ranking argument.

The source alternates Sikh/சீக்கியர் spellings and period political terminology. The English standardizes only the name form, not the historical claim.
